11. Form of testimony 
First level courts - duly subscribed written 
statements given to law enforcers or peace officers 
where the affidavits or counter-affidavits are submitted 
before the investigating prosecutor. If such are not 
available, it shall be in the form of judicial affidavit 
subject to additional to direct and cross-examination. 
 
subscribed written statements > affidavits > judicial 
affidavits 
 
Second level courts - all criminal cases where the 
demeanor of the witness is not essential in 
determining the credibility of witnesses, may be 
written 
- duly subscribed written statements given to law 
enforcers or peace officers where the affidavits or 
counter-affidavits are submitted before the 
investigating prosecutor. If such are not available, it 
shall be in the form of judicial affidavit subject to 
additional to direct and cross-examination. 
XPN: if the court determines that an eyewitness has to 
testify and the court needs to see his demeanor to 
determine his credibility, then the direct testimony 
and the cross-examination of that witness shall be 
in open court and in oral form. 
 
Section 5. Time limit following an order for new trial. — 
If the accused is to be tried again pursuant to an 
order for a new trial, the trial shall commence 
within thirty (30) days from notice of the order, 
provided that if the period becomes impractical due 
to unavailability of witnesses and other factors, the 
court may extend it but not to exceed one hundred 
eighty (180) days from notice of said order for a new 
trial. (sec. 11, cir. 38-98) 
 
Grounds for new trial 
1) Errors of law are irregularities prejudicial to the 
substantial rights of the accused has been 
committed during the trial.  
2) New and material evidence have been 
discovered which the accused could not, with 
reasonable diligence, have discovered and 
produced during the trial which when 
introduced would probably change the 
judgement. 
 
Section 6. Extended time limit. — Notwithstanding 
the provisions of section 1(g), Rule 116 and the 
preceding section 1, for the first twelve-calendar-

--- PAGE 114 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
114 
month period following its effectivity on 
September 15, 1998, the time limit with respect to 
the period from arraignment to trial imposed by said 
provision shall be one hundred eighty (180) days. 
For the second twelve-month period, the limit shall 
be one hundred twenty (120) days, and for the third 
twelve-month period, the time limit shall be eighty 
(80) days. (sec. 7, cir. 38-98) 
 
Section 7. Public attorney's duties where accused is 
imprisoned. — If the public attorney assigned to 
defend a person charged with a crime knows that 
the latter is preventively detained, either because 
he is charged with a bailable crime but has no 
means to post bail, or, is charged with a non-
bailable crime, or, is serving a term of 
imprisonment in any penal institution, it shall be 
his duty to do the following: 
 
(a) Shall promptly undertake to obtain the presence 
of the prisoner for trial or cause a notice to be served 
on the person having custody of the prisoner 
requiring such person to so advise the prisoner of 
his right to demand trial. 
(b) Upon receipt of that notice, the custodian of the 
prisoner shall promptly advise the prisoner of the 
charge and of his right to demand trial. If at anytime 
thereafter the prisoner informs his custodian that he 
demands such trial, the latter shall cause notice to 
that effect to sent promptly to the public attorney. 
(c) Upon receipt of such notice, the public attorney 
shall promptly seek to obtain the presence of the 
prisoner for trial. 
(d) When the custodian of the prisoner receives 
from the public attorney a properly supported 
request for the availability of the prisoner for 
purposes of trial, the prisoner shall be made available 
accordingly. (sec. 12, cir. 38-98) 
 
This rule just iterates the responsibility or the duty of 
the public attorney, to inform and of course, to assist 
the person accused of a crime to demand for trial. 
 
Section 8. Sanctions. — In any case in which private 
counsel for the accused, the public attorney, or the 
prosecutor. 
 
(a) Knowingly allows the case to be set for trial 
without disclosing that a necessary witness would 
be unavailable for trial; 
(b) Files a motion solely for delay which he knows 
is totally frivolous and without merit; 
(c) Makes a statement for the purpose of obtaining 
continuance which he knows to be false and 
which is material to the granting of a 
continuance; or 
(d) Willfully fails to proceed to trial without 
justification consistent with the provisions hereof, 
the court may punish such counsel, attorney, or 
prosecution, as follows: 
 
(1) By imposing on a counsel privately retained in 
connection with the defense of an accused, a fine 
not exceeding twenty thousand pesos 
(P20,000.00); 
(2) By imposing on any appointed counsel de 
oficio, public attorney, or prosecutor a fine not 
exceeding five thousand pesos (P5,000.00); and 
(3) By denying any defense counsel or prosecutor the 
right to practice before the court trying the case 
for a period not exceeding thirty (30) days. The 
punishment provided for by this section shall be 
without prejudice to any appropriate criminal 
action or other sanction authorized under these 
rules. (sec. 13, cir. 38-98) 
 
Section 9. Remedy where accused is not brought to trial 
within the time limit. — If the accused is not brought 
to trial within the time limit required by Section 1(g), 
Rule 116 and Section 1, as extended by Section 6 of 
this rule, the information may be dismissed on 
motion of the accused on the ground of denial 
of his right of speedy trial. The accused shall have 
the burden of proving the motion but the 
prosecution shall have the burden of going forward 
with the evidence to establish the exclusion of time 
under section 3 of this rule. The dismissal shall be 
subject to the rules on double jeopardy. 
 
Failure of the accused to move for dismissal prior to 
trial shall constitute a waiver of the right to 
dismiss under this section. (sec. 14, cir. 38-98) 
 
If the case is dismissed, it will be immediately final and 
executory and the first jeopardy will attach that if you 
will file the same criminal case, double jeopardy will 
arise. 
 
Burden of proof 
Accused - burden of proving the motion for dismissal 
Prosecution - burden of going forward with the 
evidence to establish the exclusion of time under 
section 3 of Rule 119 
 
Effect of failure of the accused to move for 
dismissal 
Shall constitute a waiver of the right to dismiss under 
this Section 
 
Section 10. Law on speedy trial not a bar to provision on 
speedy trial in the Constitution. — No provision of law 
on speedy trial and no rule implementing the same 
shall be interpreted as a bar to any charge of denial 
of the right to speedy trial guaranteed by section 
14(2), article III, of the 1987 Constitution. (sec. 15, 
cir. 38-98) 
 
Speedy trial - one conducted according to law, 
criminal procedure, and rules and regulations free from 
vexatious, capricious and oppressive delays. 
 
The invocation of the statutory right to speedy trial 
shall not be interpreted as a bar charge of denial of the 
constitutional right to speedy trial. 
 
Effect of the violation of the right to speedy trial  
if the accused is not brought to trial within the time 
limit, the complaint or information may be dismissed 

--- PAGE 115 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
115 
on motion of the accused on the grounds of denial of 
his right to speedy trial. 
 
Unjustified postponements 
Unjustified postponements, which prolong the trial for 
an unreasonable length of time, are what offend the 
right of the accused to speedy trial. The right to speedy 
trial allows reasonable continuance so as not to deprive 
the prosecution of its day in court. 
 
Balancing test – The test is done by counting the 
delay from the time of the filing of the information 
and the conduct of both the prosecution and the 
accused is weighed and the ff factors are considered: 
1) The length of delay 
2) Reason for the delay – most important 
3) Assertion or non-assertion of the right 
to speedy trial 
4) The prejudice that it has caused the 
accused on the delay 
 
The right to speedy trial may be raised at different 
times in the criminal proceedings 
So long as in these different times, there are different 
reasons. 
 
The Ombudsman procedures are now excluded 
from the computation of the length of delay 
Subsequently, the Supreme Court promulgated a 
jurisprudence which states that the period of 
preliminary examination/preliminary investigation in 
the Ombudsman cannot be taken into account in 
determining the length of delay of a particular criminal 
case. 
 
Section 11. Order of trial. — The trial shall proceed 
in the following order: 
 
(a) The prosecution shall present evidence to 
prove the charge and, in the proper case, the civil 
liability. 
(b) The accused may present evidence to prove 
his defense, and damages, if any, arising from the 
issuance of a provisional remedy in the case. 
(c) The prosecution and the defense may, in that 
order, present rebuttal and sur-rebuttal evidence 
unless the court, in furtherance of justice, permits 
them to present additional evidence bearing upon 
the main issue. 
(d) Upon admission of the evidence of the parties, 
the case shall be deemed submitted for decision 
unless the court directs them to argue orally or 
to submit written memoranda. 
(e) When the accused admits the act or omission 
charged in the complaint or information but 
interposes a lawful defense, the order of trial may 
be modified. (3a) 
 
In asking provisional remedies, usually the order of trial 
will not be followed. 
 
RULE 127 
Section 1. Availability of provisional remedies. — 
The provisional remedies in civil actions, insofar as 
they are applicable, may be availed of in connection 
with the civil action deemed instituted with the 
criminal action. (1a) 
 
A party is required to make his or her oral offer of 
evidence on the same day after the presentation of his 
or her last witness and the opposing party is required 
to immediately interpose his or her oral comment or 
objection thereto. 
 
Written memoranda 
In case the court directs them to file a written 
memoranda, the court will usually give the parties a 
timeframe. It would either be 15 days  
1) from notice, or  
2) from the time the last witness or last hearing  
 
Oral argument 
usually after the hearing of the oral arguments 
 
Reverse trial order 
 not mandatory 
 defense will present evidence first 
 court has the discretion 
 
Provisional remedies 
RULE 127 
Section 2. Attachment. — When the civil action is 
properly instituted in the criminal action as provided 
in Rule 111, the offended party may have the 
property of the accused attached as security for 
the satisfaction of any judgment that may be 
recovered from the accused in the following cases: 
 
(a) When the accused is about to abscond from the 
Philippines; 
(b) When the criminal action is based on a claim 
for money or property embezzled or 
fraudulently misapplied or converted to the use 
of the accused who is a public officer, officer of a 
corporation, attorney, factor, broker, agent, or clerk, 
in the course of his employment as such, or by any 
other person in a fiduciary capacity, or for a willful 
violation of duty; 
(c) When the accused has concealed, removed, or 
disposed of his property, or is about to do so; and 
(d)When the accused resides outside the 
Philippines. (2a) 
 
* since this provisional remedies will be in relation to 
the civil aspect, if it is not deemed instituted in the 
criminal case, then you cannot avail of the provisional 
remedies* 
 
Bail hearing 
Section 8. The evidence presented during the bail 
hearing shall be considered automatically 
reproduced at the trial. But upon motion of either 
party, the court may recall any witness for 
additional examination unless the latter is dead 

--- PAGE 116 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
116 
outside the Philippines or otherwise unable to 
testify. 
 
Counterclaim, cross-claim or third-party 
complaint 
No counterclaim, cross-claim or third-party 
complaint may be filed by the accused in the criminal 
case, but any cause of action which could have been 
the subject thereof may be litigated in a separate civil 
action. 
 
Non-inclusion of eyewitnesses 
The non-inclusion of some of the names of the 
eyewitnesses in the information does not preclude the 
prosecutor from presenting them during trial. The 
enumeration of witnesses in the information is not 
necessarily exhaustive. It is not meant to absolutely 
limit or lessen the prerogative of the prosecutor, 
prosecutor 
 
Participation of the judge during the trial 
Judges have as much interest as counsel in the orderly 
and expeditious presentation of evidence and have the 
duty to ask questions that would elicit the facts and the 
issues involved, clarify ambiguous remarks by 
witnesses and address the points that are overlooked 
by counsel. 
 
Our system is more of accusatory rather than 
inquisitorial. However, the questioning by the judge is 
not in any way and move to an inquisitorial form, it is 
merely to make sure that facts are clarified. He is 
allowed a reasonable leeway in putting questions to 
witnesses as may be essential to elicit relevant facts to 
make the record speak the truth. 
 
Section 16. Trial of several accused. — When two or 
more accused are jointly charged with any offense, 
they shall be tried jointly unless the court, in its 
discretion and upon motion of the prosecutor or any 
accused, orders separate trial for one or more 
accused. (8a) 
 
Rules 
1) When two or more accused or jointly charged 
in any offense, joint trial is automatic without 
need of any court order. 
2) Court may, in its discretion and upon motion 
of the prosecutor or any accused, orders 
separate trial for one or more accused. 
3) Court may grant a separate trial for those 
persons where the court has already acquired 
jurisdiction over their person. 
4) If it appears that several accused have different 
defenses, which may be antagonistic to 
each other, in the interest of justice, a separate 
trial may be granted even after the prosecution 
has finished presenting its evidence in chief. 
5) The grant or denial of a separate trial is 
discretionary upon the court and will be based 
on the circumstances of each case. 
 
Section 17. Discharge of accused to be state witness. — 
When two or more persons are jointly charged with 
the commission of any offense, upon motion of the 
prosecution before resting its case, the court may 
direct one or more of the accused to be discharged 
with their consent so that they may be witnesses for 
the state when, after requiring the prosecution to 
present evidence and the sworn statement of each 
proposed state witness at a hearing in support of the 
discharge, the court is satisfied that: 
 
(a) There is absolute necessity for the testimony 
of the accused whose discharge is requested; 
(b) The is no other direct evidence available for 
the proper prosecution of the offense committed, 
except the testimony of said accused; 
(c) The testimony of said accused can be 
substantially corroborated in its material points; 
(d) Said accused does not appear to be the most 
guilty; and 
(e) Said accused has not at any time been 
convicted of any offense involving moral 
turpitude. 
 
Evidence adduced in support of the discharge shall 
automatically form part of the trial. If the court 
denies the motion for discharge of the accused as 
state witness, his sworn statement shall be 
inadmissible in evidence. (9a) 
 
Modes of becoming a state witness 
1) Either the discharge under Section 17,  
2) By the approval of the application for 
admission into the witness protection program 
of the Department of Justice in accordance 
with Republic Act 6981. 
 
* the determination of who should be under the 
witness protection program and who should be 
discharged as witness will be the discretion of the 
prosecution. 
*whether to discharge or not is discretionary upon the court 
*the determination of who comes under the witness 
protection program solely will be with the DOJ 
 
Discharge hearing, exception 
1) If already a witness of the state under the 
witness protection program, then the provision 
of Section 17 Rule 119 will no longer apply. 
2) Where after the filing of the case in court, it 
was determined that one is a qualified person 
to be in the witness protection program. Aside 
from complying with the witness protection 
program under Republic Act 6981, Section 17 
must also be complied. 
 
Q: Is it possible that two witnesses or two accused, 
maybe discharge a state witness? 
A: Yes, so long as the factors that are provided under 
Section 17 are present. 
 
Section 18. Discharge of accused operates as acquittal. — 
The order indicated in the preceding section shall 

--- PAGE 117 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
117 
amount to an acquittal of the discharged accused 
and shall be a bar to future prosecution for the 
same offense, unless the accused fails or refuses 
to testify against his co-accused in accordance 
with his sworn statement constituting the basis 
for the discharge. (10a) 
 
Section 19. When mistake has been made in charging the 
proper offense. — When it becomes manifest at any 
time before judgment that a mistake has been 
made in charging the proper offense and the 
accused cannot be convicted of the offense 
charged or any other offense necessarily 
included therein, the accused shall not be 
discharged if there appears good cause to detain 
him. In such case, the court shall commit the 
accused to answer for the proper offense and 
dismiss the original case upon the filing of the 
proper information. (11a) 
 
Application 
This applies when the offense proved is different from 
the offense charged. And such offense proved is not 
necessarily included in the offense charged. 
 
Section 20. Appointment of acting prosecutor. — When 
a prosecutor, his assistant or deputy is disqualified to 
act due to any of the grounds stated in section 1 of 
Rule 137 or for any other reasons, the judge or the 
prosecutor shall communicate with the Secretary of 
Justice in order that the latter may appoint an acting 
prosecutor. (12a) 
 
RULE 137 
Section 1. Disqualification of judges. — No judge or 
judicial officer shall sit in any case in which he, or his 
wife or child, is pecuniarily interested as heir, legatee, 
creditor or otherwise, or in which he is related to 
either party within the sixth degree of consanguinity 
or affinity, or to counsel within the fourth degree, 
computed according to the rules of the civil law, or 
in which he has been executor, administrator, 
guardian, trustee or counsel, or in which he has been 
presided in any inferior court when his ruling or 
decision is the subject of review, without the written 
consent of all parties in interest, signed by them and 
entered upon the record.  
 
A judge may, in the exercise of his sound discretion, 
disqualify himself from sitting in a case, for just or 
valid reasons other than those mentioned above. 
 
Section 21. Exclusion of the public. — The judge may, 
motu proprio, exclude the public from the courtroom 
if the evidence to be produced during the trial is 
offensive to decency or public morals. He may also, 
on motion of the accused, exclude the public from 
the trial, except court personnel and the counsel 
of the parties. (13a) 
 
1) Court, motu proprio - exclude the public 
from the courtroom if the evidence to be 
produced during the trial is offensive to 
decency or public morals. 
2) On motion of accused - exclude the public 
from the trial, except court personnel and the 
counsel of the parties. 
 
 
Section 22. Consolidation of trials of related offenses. — 
Charges for offenses founded on the same facts or 
forming part of a series of offenses of similar 
character may be tried jointly at the discretion of 
the court. (14a) 
 
Consolidation - the act or process of uniting several 
actions into one trial or judgment by order of the 
Court where all actions are between the same parties 
pending in the same court and involving substantially 
the same issues and defenses or the court may order 
that one of the actions be tried and the other be 
decided without trial according to the judgment in the 
one selected. The purpose of consolidation is to avoid 
multiplicity of suits, guards against oppression or 
abuse, prevent delay, clear congested docket and 
simplify the work of the trial court and save 
unnecessary cost or expense. In short, the attainment 
of justice with the least expense and vexation to the 
parties and litigants. 
 
When there is duty to consolidate 
When two crimes charged in the information filed 
before two different courts where irrefutably linked 
with or related to one another as they arose from the 
same incident and were founded on the same factual 
milieu and would be proven by the same testimony of 
the same witnesses, it was held that those cases should 
have been consolidated and jointly tried in one 
branch of the court. 
 
Section 23. Demurrer to evidence. — After the 
prosecution rests its case, the court may dismiss 
the action on the ground of insufficiency of 
evidence  
(1) on its own initiative after giving the prosecution 
the opportunity to be heard or  
(2) upon demurrer to evidence filed by the 
accused with or without leave of court. 
 
If the court denies the demurrer to evidence filed 
with leave of court, the accused may adduce 
evidence in his defense. When the demurrer to 
evidence is filed without leave of court, the 
accused waives the right to present evidence and 
submits the case for judgment on the basis of the 
evidence for the prosecution. (15a) 
 
The motion for leave of court to file demurrer to 
evidence shall specifically state its grounds and 
shall be filed within a non-extendible period of five 
(5) days after the prosecution rests its case. The 
prosecution may oppose the motion within a non-
extendible period of five (5) days from its 
receipt. 
 
If leave of court is granted, the accused shall file 
the demurrer to evidence within a non-extendible 

--- PAGE 118 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
118 
period of ten (10) days from notice. The 
prosecution may oppose the demurrer to evidence 
within a similar period from its receipt. 
 
The order denying the motion for leave of court to 
file demurrer to evidence or the demurrer itself shall 
not be reviewable by appeal or by certiorari 
before judgment. (n) 
 
Demurrer to evidence 
An objection by one of the parties in an action to the 
effect that the evidence, which his adversary produce 
is insufficient in point of law, whether true or not to 
make out a case or sustained the issue. The party the 
emerging challenges the sufficiency of the whole 
evidence to sustain a verdict. 
 
A demurrer to evidence is actually a motion to dismiss 
that is filed by the accused after the prosecution has 
rested its case. Thus, demurrer to evidence file before 
the prosecution rested its case is premature. 
 
When considered sufficient 
Evidence must prove 
1) the commission of the crime and  
2) the precise degree of participation there in 
by the accused. 
 
Leave of court 
A legal term used to describe asking the court 
permission to do something that the court doesn’t 
normally allow according to its rules and procedures. 
Either party can file this motion, which is often called 
a motion for leave. 
 
Effect of leave of court 
1) demurrer with leave of court - If the court 
denies the demurrer to evidence filed with 
leave of court, the accused may adduce 
evidence in his defense. 
2) demurrer without leave of court - When the 
demurrer to evidence is filed without leave of 
court, the accused waives the right to present 
evidence and submits the case for judgment on 
the basis of the evidence for the prosecution. 
 
Period 
1) Motion for leave of court - non-extendible 
period of five (5) days after the prosecution 
rests its case. The prosecution may oppose 
the motion within a non-extendible period 
of five (5) days from its receipt. 
2) Demurrer to evidence - non-extendible 
period of ten (10) days from notice. The 
prosecution may oppose the demurrer to 
evidence within a similar period from its 
receipt. 
 
Q: What is the effect of a lack of formal offer of 
evidence during trial? 
A: documents, which may have been identified and 
marked as exhibits during the pre-trial or trial, but 
which were not formally offered in evidence cannot in 
any manner be treated as evidence. 
 
When rule of offer of evidence is relaxed 
1) the evidence was duly identified by the 
testimony duly recorded.  
2) the evidence was incorporated in the records of 
the case 
 
Based on evidence of the prosecution 
Although a demurrer to evidence must be resolved 
based on the evidence of the prosecution. There is 
nothing in the rules which would bar the court from 
taking cognizance of any other matters taken up during 
the trial or which has become part of the records of the 
case. 
 
Q: What this is a remedy against a denial of a 
demurrer? 
A: The appropriate recourse from an order denying a 
demurrer to evidence is for the court to  
1) proceed with the trial,  
2) after which the accused may file an appeal from 
the judgment of the lower court rendered after 
such trial.  
 
When such an adverse interrogatory order is rendered, 
the remedy is in absence of grave abuse of 
discretion or excess of jurisdiction, or an 
oppressive exercise of judicial authority, not to 
resort to certiorari or prohibition but to continue with 
the case in due course, and when an unfair 
favorable verdict is handed down to take an appeal 
in the manner authorized by law. 
 
1) With leave of court - present his evidence and 
thereafter take an appeal from the judgment of 
conviction 
2) Without leave of court - may interpose an 
appeal from the judgment of conviction 
rendered by the trial court after the denial of 
his demurrer. 
 
The rule generally prevailing is that certiorari does not 
lie to review a trial court's interlocutory order denying 
a demurrer to evidence. 
 
Q: What if the demurrer to evidence is filed by one 
of several accused? 
A: When one of several accused files a demurrer to 
evidence with leave of court. It is sound practice for 
the trial court to resolve the demurrer to evidence 
before proceeding with a trial for the other accused. 
The rationale for this is that the accused filing of the 
demurrer does not waive his right to present this 
evidence in the event of the denial of his demurrer. 
 
Section 24. Reopening. — At any time before finality 
of the judgment of conviction, the judge may, 
motu proprio or upon motion, with hearing in 
either case, reopen the proceedings to avoid a

119 
miscarriage of justice. The proceedings shall be 
terminated within thirty (30) days from the order 
grating it. (n) 
 
Reopening of a case - one to permit the introduction 
of new evidence and practically to permit a new trial. 
 
Period 
Any time before the judgment of conviction becomes 
final. This presupposes a situation where the judgment 
has been promulgated in the proceedings shall be 
terminated within 30 days from the order granting the 
reopening. 
 
Who may file 
1) May be filed by either party 
2) The reopening based on the rules may also be 
made at the instance of the court. But in any 
case, there must be a hearing conducted for the 
purpose of determining the propriety of such 
reopening. 
3) A case may be open before the rendition of 
judgment without the consent of the 
accused. 
 
Motion for new trial Motion for reopening 
may only be filed by the 
accused or granted with 
his consent 
May be had on the 
motion by either party or 
motu proprio by the 
court. 
governed by Rule 121 of 
the Rules 
governed by Section 24, 
Rule 119 
may be applied for and 
granted only upon 
specific well-defined 
grounds, 
controlled by no other 
rule than that of the 
paramount interests of 
justice, that is to avoid a 
miscarriage of justice. 
 
RULE 120 
JUDGMENT 
 
Section 1. Judgment definition and form. — Judgment is 
the adjudication by the court that the accused is 
guilty or not guilty of the offense charged and the 
imposition on him of the proper penalty and civil 
liability, if any. It must be written in the official 
language, personally and directly prepared by the 
judge and signed by him and shall contain clearly and 
distinctly a statement of the facts and the law upon 
which it is based. (1a) 
 
Judgment - final consideration in determination of a 
court of competent jurisdiction upon the matters 
submitted to it in an action or proceeding. 
 
It is the adjudication by the court that the accused is 
guilty or not guilty of the offense charged and the 
imposition on him of the proper penalty and civil 
liability, if any. 
 
Form 
1) written in the official language 
2) personally and directly prepared by the judge 
and signed by him - To be valid, the judgment 
must be signed and promulgated during the 
incumbency of the judge who signed the same. 
 
The efficacy of the decision or the judgment is not 
necessarily impaired by the fact that the ponente, the 
one who pens the judgment only took over from 
another judge who had earlier presided over the trial, 
for it does not follow that a judge who was not 
present during the trial cannot render a valid and just 
decision. 
 
3) contain clearly and distinctly a statement of the 
facts and the law upon which it is based 
 
Function 
1) to inform the parties of their case, or their 
reasons for the decision so that if any of them 
appeals, he can point out to the appellate court 
the findings of facts or their rulings on points 
of law which he disagrees with. 
2) assurance to the parties that in reaching a 
judgment, the judge did so through the process 
of legal reasoning. 
 
Date of submission 
The 1987 Constitution provides under Section 15 (1), 
Article 8, that unless reduced by the Supreme Court, all 
lower courts other than a collegial court must decide or 
resolve matters within three months from the date of 
submission.  
 
Submission - Upon filing of last pleading 
 
When judgment was rendered beyond the three-
month period 
Those decisions or judgment are valid. However, it will 
be the judges that failed to render the decision within 
that period who will be sanctioned. The rendition of 
the judgment beyond the periods provided for under 
the Constitution will not invalidate the judgment. 
 
Cases under the Rule on Summary Procedure 
30 days following the receipt of the last affidavit 
and position paper or the expiration of the period for 
filing the same within which to render the judgment. 
 
Drug cases 
The decision shall be rendered by the trial court within 
15 days from the date of the submission of the case 
for resolution. 
 
Section 2. Contents of the judgment. — If the judgment 
is of conviction, it shall state  
(1) the legal qualification of the offense 
constituted by the acts committed by the accused 
and the aggravating or mitigating circumstances 
which attended its commission;  
(2) the participation of the accused in the offense, 
whether as principal, accomplice, or accessory after 
the fact;  

--- PAGE 120 ---
  FROM THE ANNOTATIONS OF DEAN TAN AND DISCUSSIONS OF ATTY DERIJE 
 
            Criminal Procedure | VENTEROSO 
120 
(3) the penalty imposed upon the accused; and  
(4) the civil liability or damages caused by his 
wrongful act or omission to be recovered from the 
accused by the offended party, if there is any, unless 
the enforcement of the civil liability by a separate 
civil action has been reserved or waived. 
 
In case the judgment is of acquittal, it shall state 
whether the evidence of the prosecution absolutely 
failed to prove the guilt of the accused or merely 
failed to prove his guilt beyond reasonable doubt. In 
either case, the judgment shall determine if the act 
or omission from which the civil liability might arise 
did not exist. (2a) 
 
Proof beyond reasonable doubt 
To be considered sufficient, evidence must prove:  
1) The commission of the crime; and  
2) The precise degree of the participation of the 
accused therein. 
 
GR: Conviction must be by virtue of direct evidence 
XPN: Supreme Court has pronounced that conviction 
of an accused through circumstantial evidence as long 
as the following requisites or presence may be had:  
(1) That there must be more than one circumstance;  
(2) That the inference must be based on proven facts; 
and  
(3) That the combination of all circumstances 
produces a conviction beyond doubt of the guilt of 
the accused 
 
Proof of minority 
The allegation of minority or age must be proved with equal 
certainty and clearness as the crime itself. 
 
Judgment of acquittal 
In case the judgment is for acquittal, it shall state whether 
the evidence of the prosecution absolutely failed to prove 
the guilt of the accused or merely failed to prove this guilt 
beyond reasonable doubt. In either case, the judgment shall 
determine if the act or omission from which the civil 
liability might arise did not exist. 
 
Two kinds of acquittal 
1) An acquittal on the ground the accused is not the 
author of the act or omission complained of. 
2) An acquittal based on reasonable doubt of the guilt 
of the accused. 
 
Finality of acquittal rule 
A judgment of acquittal is immediately final and executory, 
and it is not appealable. It cannot be reconsidered, nor can 
it be modified, except to eliminate something which is civil 
or administrative in nature, or it can be modified so long as 
it is favorable to the accused. 
 
1) Judgment of conviction – 15 days to appeal 
from the date of promulgation of the judgment 
2) Judgment of acquittal – becomes final and 
executory 
 
Dismissal upon an affidavit of assistance 
GR: An affidavit of desistance by itself, is not a ground for 
the dismissal of a criminal case, after the institution of the 
action.
